# Philippines payroll obligation inventory

**Review date:** 29 September 2026. **Payroll periods:** 1 December 2025 onward. **Disposition:** open; no Philippines-wide certification. This is the Philippines obligation register. The IDs below are durable provision references; a row records a requirement or a source disposition, not a claim that its mechanism works for every case. An unreviewed provision, amendment, region, worker class, historical input or saved-output path remains in scope.

## Sealed rule state

**PH-WG01 reduced-class proof guard (29 September 2026; pending combined gate):** The saved selector now requires the dated terms revision to carry a worksite municipality and sector reference plus attached source files before it can apply a PH private wage class below the ordinary `NCR` or `IV-A` key. Any worker-count branch also requires the dated company-fact revision to carry an establishment/count reference and attachment; the current company row alone cannot supply the reduction. The exact municipality and sector, single-establishment declaration, integer headcount, sealed class row and settings version in force on each day must agree. Focused refusal probes remove each source, remove the dated company revision, and cover Cavite/Rosario agriculture. These files are user-supplied evidence pointers, not authenticated municipal or industry determinations; a false attachment remains an input-integrity risk. The reduced-class guard and UI source fields have not passed the combined gate.

**PH-SS02 evidence UI (29 September 2026; pending combined gate):** The HR Controller Leave page now exposes company-scoped maternity cases, paid SSS month history, candidate advance plans with an `advance_status` query, cutoff premium evidence and separate employee-cash/reimbursement movements. Plan and cutoff create forms show only writable evidence inputs; their existing records display frozen values as read-only. The status labels documented cash as a recorded attachment, not an authenticated bank payment, SSS award or settled payroll. An older plan is rejected by the status query when superseded. Cash recorded before any plan cannot be retroactively placed into a frozen candidate; its migration/reconciliation remains an in-product gap. These UI paths have not passed the combined gate and do not release the maternity payroll refusal.

**PH-SS02 plan-source integrity (29 September 2026; pending combined gate):** A saved advance plan now requires a contribution-statement attachment for each of its twelve source months, including zero-credit months. Both the advance-status query and a new employee cash movement compare current saved month IDs, regular MSCs, payment dates and references with the plan's frozen snapshot and refuse a changed source until a corrected plan revision exists. File presence is checked, but attachment contents and authenticity remain unverified. Existing historical cash with no plan still has no safe automatic migration.

`seed/jurisdiction/PH/` now has six active sealed versions covering consecutive intervals from 1 December 2025 and 18 voided historical versions; six of the latter were replaced by the 26 September SSS age correction. The operative boundaries are 1 December 2025, 1 January 2026, 6 January 2026, 7 February 2026, 1 April 2026 and 26 September 2026. The scheme catalogue has SSS, SSS_MPF, SSS_EC, PHIC, HDMF and WTAX in each active version. A structural read found 24 settings and no orphan `settings_id` among 1,244 child rows across all six catalogues. A sealed profile proves only that a dated configuration exists; it does not prove complete legal scope. The wage-floor seed includes selected uniform regional private and domestic rates and bounded exact worksite classes across multiple regions; other locations, regional classes, mid-interval cutovers and saved payroll behavior remain unproved.

**PH registration-status audit (29 September 2026; seed-only):** All 36 SSS, SSS_MPF, SSS_EC, PHIC, HDMF and WTAX rows in the six active sealed versions explicitly set `unregistered_action: ASSESS`, so a recorded `NOT_REGISTERED` status does not waive a positive assessment. This is consistent with [SSS employer guidance](https://www.sss.gov.ph/employer-er/), [PhilHealth employer payment guidance](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/partners/employers/pay_procedures.php), [HDMF Circular 275](https://www.pagibigfund.gov.ph/document/pdf/circulars/provident/HDMF%20Circular%20275%20-%20Implementing%20Guidelines%20on%20Employer%20Registration%20Contribution%20and%20Remittance.pdf) and [BIR 1601-C instructions](https://efps.bir.gov.ph/efps-war/EFPSWeb_war/help/help1601c.html). Twelve earlier, voided settings snapshots omit the field and therefore retain the model’s `SKIP` default; they are historical and are not selected by the current in-force timeline. Historical saved-payroll replay against a voided settings ID still needs a separate runtime/data audit.

**PH-WG01/10/24 worksite-class pass (29 September 2026; bounded):** The six active versions have `wages.classified_by_worksite` rows tied to an exact dated `employment_terms.worksite`, dated `worksite_sector` and, where size matters, dated company facts `ph_wage_one_establishment` and `ph_wage_worker_count` without seed defaults. The class selector refuses an unlisted worksite, absent sector/count, multiple matching classes, multiple establishments for a size branch, or a class/count change inside one pay window. The signed [NCR-26](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/01.-Wage-Order-No.-NCR-26.pdf), [NCR-28](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Wage-Order-No.-NCR-28.pdf) and [IVA-22](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Wage-Order-No.-IVA-22_compressed.pdf) support the 161 NCR/IV-A rows per version. NCR coverage names all 17 LGUs: Caloocan, Las Piñas, Makati, Malabon, Mandaluyong, Manila, Marikina, Muntinlupa, Navotas, Parañaque, Pasay, Pasig, Quezon City, San Juan, Taguig, Valenzuela and Pateros, each under `NCR/`. The IV-A sample is exactly `Cavite/Bacoor`, `Cavite/Rosario`, `Batangas/Rosario`, `Cavite/Noveleta`, `Cavite/Amadeo`, `Laguna/Calamba` and `Rizal/Cainta`. Cavite/Rosario carries its special ₱600 nonagriculture class; Batangas/Rosario uses the ordinary first-class rate. Small retail/service (≤10), reclassified first-class and agricultural April 2026 tranches are represented for those sampled sites. All other IV-A municipalities and named zones, including the LIMA Technology Center within Batangas/Malvar, are unseeded; a municipality-only Malvar key would be unsafe. NCR-27 litigation, exemptions, nonmonthly and piece-rate equivalence, all other private regional classes and saved payroll outputs remain open. Seven focused classification tests and 146 PH-targeted tests pass. Neither result certifies live tenant behavior.

**PH-WG01 exact uniform-region extension (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** The [signed Region II order RTWPB 2-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Wage-Order-No.-RTWPB-2-24.pdf) and the [official regional city list](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-ii/) support exact sites `II/Tuguegarao`, `II/Cauayan`, `II/Ilagan`, `II/Santiago` at ₱500/day across private sectors and ₱6,500/month domestic from before 1 December 2025. The [CAR-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Wage-Order-No.-CAR-24.pdf) and [CAR regional page](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/car/) support `CAR/Baguio` at ₱505/day and ₱6,600/month domestic from 30 December 2025. [MIMAROPA-13](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Wage-Order-No.-MIMAROPA-13.pdf) and the [IV-B regional page](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-ivb/) support `IV-B/Calapan` and `IV-B/Puerto Princesa` at ₱455/day and ₱7,000/month domestic from 1 January 2026. [RBV-22](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Wage-Order-No.-RBV-22.pdf) and the [V regional page](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-v/) support `V/Iriga`, `V/Legazpi`, `V/Ligao`, `V/Masbate`, `V/Naga`, `V/Sorsogon`, `V/Tabaco` at ₱435/day only in complete sealed intervals before 8 April 2026; the domestic ₱6,000/month remains seeded. [RXIII-19/20](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-xiii/) support `XIII/Bayugan`, `XIII/Bislig`, `XIII/Butuan`, `XIII/Cabadbaran`, `XIII/Surigao`, `XIII/Tandag` at the already sealed ₱435/₱455/₱475 complete-interval rates and ₱6,000/₱6,500 domestic rates. These 20 exact sites add 100 rows to each of six active classifiers (261 uniform/NCR/IV-A rows per version), with no overlap. A row whose version lacks a lawfully complete rate still refuses: CAR and IV-B private in December; XIII at the January and May cutovers; V from 8 April onward. The remaining municipalities, unseeded private classes and exact mid-version effectivity dates remain open. A region name or a claimed city string alone is not independent proof of the employee’s physical worksite; saved data validation remains open.

**PH-WG12 Region III bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** The [signed RBIII-26](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Wage-Order-No.-RBIII-26.pdf) and [NWPC Region III schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-iii/) support exact `Pampanga/San Fernando` only. The six active classifiers now carry five rows each for this worksite: `AGRICULTURE` uses ₱540/day before 16 April 2026 and ₱570 afterward; `RETAIL_SERVICE` uses ₱560 then ₱590; `MANUFACTURING` and `OTHER_NONAGRI` use ₱570 then ₱600; `DOMESTIC` uses ₱6,500/month. The active settings before 16 April and beginning 26 September contain the applicable private keys. The 1 April–25 September version spans the 16 April tranche and lacks a single safe private payable rate, so selection refuses there pending a dated rule boundary. The five rows bring the active class catalogue to **266 rows per version before the Region I extension**. Other Region III worksites, Aurora classes, exact tranche selection, order exceptions and saved behavior remain open.

**PH-WG30 Region I bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed RB I-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Wage-Order-No.-RB-1-24.pdf) and the [NWPC Region I schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-i/) give ₱480/day for agriculture and nonagriculture with fewer than 10 employees, and ₱505/day for nonagriculture with 10 or more, effective 19 November 2025. The official city list supports exact `I/Alaminos`, `I/Batac`, `I/Candon`, `I/Dagupan`, `I/Laoag`, `I/San Carlos`, `I/San Fernando`, `I/Urdaneta` and `I/Vigan`. Each gets one agriculture row, paired 0–9/10+ rows for retail/service, manufacturing and other nonagriculture, and one domestic row using the already seeded ₱6,700/month under [RB I-DW-06](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Wage-Order-No.-RB-1-DW-06.pdf). This adds 72 class rows to each active version, **338 rows per version before the Region XII extension**. The sector, exact worksite and single-establishment worker count must be dated; absent/ambiguous facts refuse. Other municipalities, exemptions, saved selection and pay-basis equivalence remain open.

**PH-WG29/37 Region XII bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed RB XII-25](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/Wage-Order-No.-RBXII-25.pdf) and the [NWPC Region XII schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-xii/) support ₱450/day nonagriculture and ₱433 agriculture from 2 November 2025, then ₱460/₱443 from 15 December 2025. This adds 20 rows/version, **358 total before the Region XI extension**. The 63 North Cotabato barangays transferred to BARMM are unseeded; neither a broad province code nor a region fallback may select these rates. **PH-WG16/17 Region XI bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** The [NWPC Region XI schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-xi/), signed [XI-23](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Wage-Order-No.-RB-XI-23.pdf), signed [XI-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Wage-Order-No.-RB-XI-24.pdf) and [XI-DW-04](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/Wage-Order-No.-RB-XI-DW-04.pdf) support six exact sites: `XI/Davao`, `XI/Digos`, `XI/Island Garden of Samal`, `XI/Mati`, `XI/Panabo` and `XI/Tagum`. Agriculture/nonagriculture use ₱505/₱510 in the three complete pre-13 March 2026 active intervals and ₱525/₱540 from 26 September onward after the 1 September second tranche. The 7 February–31 March and 1 April–25 September versions straddle a tranche and have no payable private XI key. Domestic ₱6,500/month is selectable from 1 April onward; earlier intervals have no XI domestic key. These 30 rows/version bring the active classifiers to **388 rows/version before the Region VIII slice**. Other sites, mid-interval effectivity, exemptions and saved output remain open.

**PH-WG27 Region VIII bounded slice (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed VIII-25](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Wage-Order-No.-RB-VIII-25.pdf) and the [NWPC Region VIII schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-viii/) support exact `VIII/Calbayog`, `VIII/Catbalogan`, `VIII/Baybay`, `VIII/Borongan`, `VIII/Maasin`, `VIII/Ormoc` and `VIII/Tacloban` for agriculture and retail/service only. Agriculture and retail/service with 1–10 workers use ₱422/day from 8 December 2025, then ₱440 from 1 June 2026; retail/service with 11+ uses ₱452 then ₱470. These rates are sealed in the three complete 1 January–31 March 2026 intervals and the 26 September onward interval. The December interval spans 8 December and April–September spans 1 June, so both refuse private VIII class selection. Cottage/handicraft and ordinary nonagriculture need a distinct sector input; no manufacturing or `OTHER_NONAGRI` row was guessed. The 21 new rows/version bring the active classifiers to **409 rows/version before the Region X extension**. Domestic worker area classes, other worksites, exceptions and saved output remain open.

**PH-WG15/23 Region X bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** Signed [RX-23](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/Wage-Order-No.-RX-23_compressed.pdf), [RX-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Wage-Order-No.-RX-24.pdf), [RBX-DW-06](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Wage-Order-No.-RBX-DW-06.pdf) and the [NWPC Region X schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-x/) identify fifteen exact Category I sites: cities Cagayan de Oro, Iligan, Malaybalay, Valencia, Gingoog, El Salvador, Ozamiz; municipalities Tagoloan, Villanueva, Jasaan, Opol, Maramag, Quezon, Manolo Fortich, Lugait, each keyed as `X/<name>`. Agriculture, manufacturing and other nonagriculture use Category I, while retail/service with at most 10 workers uses Category II and 11+ uses I. In complete sealed intervals, I/II are ₱461/₱446 per day before 16 January 2026, ₱486/₱471 from 7 February–31 March, and ₱500/₱485 from 26 September onward; the January 6 and April 1 versions straddle the January 16 and May 1 tranches and lack payable X private keys. Domestic uses ₱6,000/month before 16 January and ₱6,500 from 7 February onward; January 6 straddles that change and has no X domestic key. Ninety new rows/version bring the active classifiers to **499 rows/version before the Region IX extension**. Category II other-area sites, exemptions, exact cutover dates and saved output remain open.

**PH-WG14/22 Region IX bounded class (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed RIX-24](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Wage-Order-No.-RIX-24.pdf), [signed RIX-DW-06](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Wage-Order-No.-RIX-DW-06.pdf) and the [NWPC Region IX schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-ix/) identify five exact cities: `IX/Dapitan`, `IX/Dipolog`, `IX/Isabela`, `IX/Pagadian`, `IX/Zamboanga`. December predates RIX-24 and April–September straddles 1 June, so those intervals have no private IX key. Sulu’s territorial transition, other municipalities, exemptions, exact tranche dates and saved output remain open.

**PH-WG32 Region VI bounded slice (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed RBVI-29](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Wage-Order-No.-RBVI-29.pdf), [signed RBVI-DW-07](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Wage-Order-No.-RBVI-DW-07.pdf) and the [NWPC Region VI schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-vi/) support exact `VI/Iloilo City` from before 1 December 2025: agriculture ₱520/day, nonagriculture employing at most 10 workers ₱525, nonagriculture employing 11+ ₱550, and domestic ₱6,500/month. Its eight class rows in every active version bring the catalogue to **537 rows/version before the Region VII slice**. Negros Occidental/Bacolod and Negros Oriental/Dumaguete need the NIR transition rule and correct continuing board order; this slice deliberately avoids them. Other Region VI worksites, exemptions and saved output remain open.

**PH-WG33 Region VII bounded slice and announced next order (29 September 2026; seed-only, untested):** [Signed ROVII-26](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Wage-Order-No.-ROVII-26.pdf), [ROVII-DW-05](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Wage-Order-No.-ROVII-DW-05.pdf) and the [NWPC Region VII schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-vii/) support exact `VII/Cebu` at the Class A ₱540/day private and ₱7,000/month domestic floors in the five complete sealed intervals before 26 September 2026. Five rows/version bring the active class catalogue to **542 rows/version**. The board’s newly published [ROVII-27 and ROVII-DW-06 schedule](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-vii/) states ₱582/day Class A and ₱7,500/month domestic **from 14 October 2026**, a future date at review. The 26 September open-ended version contains that future change, so it has no payable VII private or domestic key; exact date-versioning must be added before the future rate can be assessed. The board says Negros Oriental and Siquijor remain covered under VII’s Class B despite the NIR formation, but no Negros site was seeded without the full territorial transition review. Other VII sites, exemptions and saved output remain open.

**Verified correction PH-SS01:** [RA 11199 s.9(a)](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/20190207-RA-11199-RRD.pdf) and the [SSS implementing rules](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/IRR-RA11199-SS-Act-of-2018_2.pdf) make age relevant to initial compulsory employee coverage. A July 2026 PHP30,000 wage with an empty birth date previously completed with zero SSS/MPF and a warning. All six replacement versions now refuse that payable assessment until the birth date is recorded. `tests/ph-sss-unknown-age.probe.test.ts` reproduced the missing refusal before replacement and passes afterward; 25 focused PH tests pass. This does not establish the other SSS eligibility and historical branches.

**PH-HR09 seed and production-path correction (29 September 2026):** [RA 11210 §§3, 5](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/86567) grants a pregnant private-sector employee 105 days' maternity leave with full pay (60 days for miscarriage or emergency termination, plus 15 days for a qualified solo parent). Section 5(a)'s three paid SSS contributions in the twelve months before the contingency semester determine the SSS cash benefit; months since SSS registration do not establish that payment history and cannot deny the leave itself. The 24 PH `MATERNITY_LEAVE` catalogue rows no longer gate leave on `facts.SSS.since_months >= 3`; their authority text now separates the two questions, and `entitlement.calendar_days` charges rest days within a continuous leave span. `tests/ph-maternity-entitlement.test.ts` now approves a birth-event leave day through the real planner and runs payroll twice: a female employee with one month since SSS registration, and the same employee without SSS registration. Both retain ₱20,000 gross salary and a paid maternity leave day. A second focused probe approves one 105-day birth event from 5 October 2026 to 17 January 2027 as four entries split at the monthly pay cutoff: all 105 calendar dates, including Sundays, are charged once; each of four payroll periods captures only its own entry and retains ₱20,000 gross. This tests the seeded calendar-day path in a synthetic world. The planner also accepts a standalone one-day birth entry, so it does not yet require the complete uninterrupted statutory span across entries. A later approval beyond day 105 now requires a birth-matched case and event-valid solo-parent LGU document; that boundary awaits the final gate. Holiday, allocation, live saved tenant and unusual pay-basis cases remain open. SSS cash benefit, employer advance, salary differential and paid-contribution history remain open under PH-SS02/R16.

The same 24 rows now accept the explicit `EMERGENCY_TERMINATION` event and route it to 60 days, as required by [RA 11210 §§3, 5](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/86567) and its [implementing rules](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/96742). The event input is free text in the current editor; this does not establish classification, proof, the full 60-day emergency-termination path or saved payroll behavior. Those remain open under PH-HR09/R06.

**PH-SS02 bounded cash calculation (29 September 2026; not run):** The new person-level `sss_contribution_months` collection records each coverage month, regular-program MSC up to ₱20,000, actual payment date, and SSS statement reference, including explicit zero-credit months and prior-employer evidence. A pure PH calculator refuses an incomplete or duplicate twelve-month history, counts only contributions paid before the contingency semester, requires three paid months, selects the six highest regular MSCs, divides by 180 for ADSC and multiplies by 60/105/120 days. The [SSS maternity page, Qualifying Conditions and Benefit Computation](https://www.sss.gov.ph/maternity-benefit/) expressly excludes contributions paid within or after the contingency semester, even for a qualifying coverage month. The calculated peso amount is a candidate, rounded to centavos at the final multiplication; an actual SSS award and the agency's rounding method are not authenticated. A saved case can now request a nonauthoritative `sss_candidate` from that employee's full statement history; the query selects the event's twelve-month window, refuses missing months and unproved solo-parent claims, and does not fund a payslip or employer advance. The [2024 DOLE handbook](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Workers-Statutory-Monetary-Benefits-Handbook-2024-Edition.pdf) says the differential counts in rank-and-file 13th-month basic salary; [BIR RMC 105-2019](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RMC%20105-2019.pdf) says it is exempt from income and withholding taxes. The handbook’s shorthand three-contribution condition belongs to the SSS cash benefit: RA 11210 §3 grants leave to covered female workers, while §5(a) attaches three paid months to the SSS daily benefit. The leave gate therefore does not use SSS registration age or contribution months. [DOLE Advisory 01-2019](https://dole.gov.ph/news/da-01-19-guidelines-on-the-computation-of-salary-differential-of-female-workers-during-her-maternity-leave-and-its-criteria-for-exemption-pursuant-to-republic-act-no-11210-and-its-implementing-rules/) (linked by the [DOLE FOI response](https://www.foi.gov.ph/requests/inquiry-on-salary-differential-maternity/)) sets a full-pay, employee-premium and actual-SSS-benefit sequence for the differential; the current payroll has not implemented that sequence, while the government-hosted PDF remains inaccessible; a [signed DOLE original scan mirrored by PALSCON](https://palscon.org/public/assets/img/labor-laws-advisory/DA-01-19-Guidelines-on-the-Computation-of-Salary-Differential-of-Female-Workers-during-her-Maternity.pdf) has the filename identified by DOLE FOI. DOLE Advisory 01-A-2019 and BIR RMC 105-2019 later exempted the differential from income and withholding taxes. Seven-day caregiver allocation, saved payroll funding/reimbursement, exemption validation and differential settlement remain open. PH-SS02 remains open for wage and cash integration.

**PH-SS02 case, cash and saved-evidence slice (29 September 2026; not run):** `ph_maternity_cases` records a stable employee/employment application reference and date, expected delivery, actual contingency kind/date, planned leave span, SSS notification, actual SSS award, and a claimed DOLE differential exemption with an effective range and approval reference. It can open before childbirth so the [SSS 30-day employer advance clock](https://www.sss.gov.ph/maternity-benefit/) is not lost. `ph_maternity_movements` separately records dated employer SSS advances, employer salary-differential payments, and SSS reimbursement receipts; reimbursement is employer money, never employee net pay. HR Manager, HR Controller and Senior Management alone have record and reconciliation-query access; neither collection offers delete. The new read-only `ph_maternity_cases.reconcile` query compares an exact employment/event kind/date with approved, unreversed `MATERNITY_LEAVE` charge dates, requires a continuous 105-day birth span, or 120 days with event-specific solo-parent document evidence (at least 60 days after birth), or 60 days from miscarriage/ETP, and reports paid payslip capture of every matched cutoff entry. It separately reports the application-plus-30-day deadline, actual SSS award, advances paid by the deadline, and employer reimbursement receipts; it flags receipts exceeding the lesser of award and recorded advance. It does **not** certify gross/full wage, automatically pay or withhold, determine a valid DOLE exemption, calculate differential or incorporate it in 13th-month pay. No case FK exists on leave entries: exact-event matching is a read-only association and cannot prove a user entered the true event. The 120-day solo-parent case, leave-approval and pre-payroll recheck branches require a birth-specific claim and LGU document file, kind, issuer, reference, issue and validity dates, and explicit review of the required signatories and certificate details, with the SSS six-month document-issue exception for first-time solo parents; seven-day caregiver allocation remains unassessed. Stored evidence is not independently authenticated. A complete span is reported only after all dated charges exist, so lawful future cutoff-split submissions remain possible; approval itself still accepts an incomplete span. Source references are entered evidence, not independently authenticated. Full award/payment/DOLE approval files, wage basis, payout reconciliation and reimbursement claim controls remain open.

**PH-SS02 saved payroll double-payment guard (29 September 2026; not run):** Ordinary PH maternity payroll already retains the full BASIC salary through approved paid leave; [BIR RMC 105-2019](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RMC%20105-2019.pdf) describes full pay as the SSS benefit plus employer differential, not those two cash transfers on top of full salary. Before a payroll run is written, `assertPhMaternityPayrollCashSafe` now reads cases and cash movements for the employments whose wages this run would pay. It refuses a salary window overlapping a cash-funded case's planned leave span **even when the leave entry is missing, awaiting approval or already pinned**. If an employee cash movement exists but the case has no planned span, all payroll for that employment refuses until the span is entered. Reimbursement receipts alone do not block employee payroll, and unrelated employments or salary windows are unaffected. Employee-cash movements now freeze the planned leave span at payment. The payroll guard checks both that snapshot and the current span, so correcting dates after a prebirth advance cannot hide the original cash-covered period. Once an actual event is recorded it cannot be changed after employee cash; older cash rows lacking a snapshot still lock changes to a recorded span. The refusal is a safety boundary, not completed payroll: the product still lacks a saved, case-linked prior-cash offset allocated across cutoff payslips; therefore it cannot settle this required scenario. A later cash movement can still be recorded after an earlier payslip was paid; the reconciliation then reports `CASH_AND_PAID_PAYROLL_UNRECONCILED` for paid maternity entries. No additional earning or `NON_WAGE_PAYMENT` line was added, because either would overpay the employee and a reimbursement would incorrectly enter employee net pay. The case's full wage, 13th-month basis, contribution treatment and legally valid exemption remain unassessed.

The prewrite guard also now refuses every approved, unpinned PH maternity charge overlapping a salary window when there is no saved case-priced allocation, including cases with no recorded advance. This closes the ordinary taxable BASIC path while the case allocator is unsaved. The earlier synthetic PH-HR09 payroll probe expecting unchanged BASIC must be revised once a lawful saved path exists; it is no longer a valid settlement expectation.

The untested pre-settlement maternity guard also rejects missing event evidence and duplicate, fractional or gapped approved calendar charges in the salary window. Miscarriage and emergency termination must start on the contingency date and stop at 60 days; birth still stops at 105 or an evidenced 120 days. [RA 11210](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/86567) requires continuous, uninterrupted leave; [SSS guidance](https://www.sss.gov.ph/maternity-benefit/) specifies 60 days without prenatal leave for miscarriage or emergency termination. A partial future span can still be approved, so the guard does not establish the complete award before the last cutoff.

Once employee maternity cash is recorded, the application date and case reference are also locked. Those fields identify the 30-day advance deadline and the funded application; editing either after payment would rewrite the reconciliation history. This guard awaits the final gate.

**PH-SS02 exact-evidence assessment (29 September 2026; gate pending):** The saved `ph_maternity_cases.assess_cash_evidence` query now refuses an absent actual SSS award document/amount/date/reference, an absent single referenced PHP `employment_wage_periods.normal_wages` record whose inclusive dates equal the entire planned maternity span, incomplete approved continuous leave, or a paid maternity payslip without a saved SSS/full-wage/prior-cash allocation. When those checks pass it exposes recorded full-span normal wages, SSS award, employee advance and differential cash, and employer reimbursement separately with `ALLOCATION_UNASSESSED`. This is a strict evidence review, not a differential calculation, cash settlement, exemption grant, proof of document authenticity, or permission to resume payroll after an advance. The exact-span wage requirement can reject otherwise lawful cases whose existing wage evidence is split by month; no prorating or summation is guessed. The [SSS maternity guidance](https://www.sss.gov.ph/maternity-benefit/) defines full pay as actual SSS cash plus employer differential over the entire leave and requires employer advance; the [DOLE handbook](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Workers-Statutory-Monetary-Benefits-Handbook-2024-Edition.pdf) includes the differential in rank-and-file 13th-month basic salary. The [signed DOLE DA 01-2019 scan](https://palscon.org/public/assets/img/labor-laws-advisory/DA-01-19-Guidelines-on-the-Computation-of-Salary-Differential-of-Female-Workers-during-her-Maternity.pdf) now establishes the formula, while actual statutory premium inputs, case-linked saved cutoff split, 13th-month accumulation and prior-cash offset remain in-product blockers. Payroll continues to refuse a cash-funded overlapping salary period.

**PH-SS02 DOLE formula and pure cutoff ledger (29 September 2026; gate pending):** A signed [DOLE Department Advisory 01-2019](https://palscon.org/public/assets/img/labor-laws-advisory/DA-01-19-Guidelines-on-the-Computation-of-Salary-Differential-of-Female-Workers-during-her-Maternity.pdf), dated 9 July 2019 and bearing the Secretary's signature on page 4, was inspected in full (four advisory pages and three form pages); the [DOLE FOI reply](https://www.foi.gov.ph/requests/inquiry-on-salary-differential-maternity/) identifies the same PDF filename. Its §II defines full pay as monthly salary × maternity calendar days/30, then subtracts the employee SSS, PhilHealth and Pag-IBIG premium shares covering leave and the actual SSS benefit to obtain the employer differential. Its worked ₱31,300 monthly/105-day case yields ₱109,550 full pay, ₱4,656.31 premium shares, ₱70,000 SSS and ₱34,893.69 differential; the negative example has no positive employer differential. §IV includes only the employer differential in rank-and-file 13th-month basic. §III's original taxable statement is superseded by [BIR RMC 105-2019](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RMC%20105-2019.pdf), which exempts the differential from income and withholding taxes. New pure `calculatePhMaternityPay` and `allocatePhMaternityPay` functions reproduce the examples, require exact centavo inputs and a continuous 60/105/120-day cutoff partition, distribute the actual SSS award and nonnegative employer differential with centavo conservation, offset recorded employee cash once by payment date, and split the differential's 13th basic by covered calendar year. A cutoff whose recorded premiums would produce a negative local differential refuses pending a supported treatment. This is **not yet a saved payroll mechanism**: the run does not supply exact maternity premium shares, pin a case-linked allocation, reduce employee net for prior cash, or replace full BASIC with differential-only 13th basic. The existing cash-funded payroll refusal remains until those paths are wired and verified. These pure tests were authored but not run in this concurrent pass.

The pure `planPhMaternityPayslips` adapter now accepts an event-matched saved case, all approved leave entries, existing payslips, actual cash movements, an exact full-span PHP normal-wage row plus referenced monthly salary, and cutoff contexts with evidenced premium shares. It refuses a future-dated SSS award, conflicting prior paid salary window, any already pinned maternity entry even if its payslip row was omitted, overlapping cross-event maternity charges, an incomplete or duplicated entry-to-cutoff assignment, missing premium evidence, or disagreement between recorded full-span wages and the DOLE formula. Its per-cutoff proposal offsets SSS advance only against the SSS component and differential cash only against the differential component. Cash after the first proposed cutoff payday refuses until an earlier settled payslip can be reconciled; the unsaved adapter has no such history. The [SSS maternity page, Manner of Benefit Payment](https://www.sss.gov.ph/maternity-benefit/) requires the full employer advance within 30 days of application, so the adapter's narrow payable proposal additionally requires actual SSS advance and positive differential evidence in full by both that deadline and the first proposed payday. Later cutoff payroll alone cannot cure a missed advance. It returns frozen proposed per-cutoff gross/SSS/premium/differential/prior-cash/net-offset/13th-year details marked `PROPOSED_NOT_SAVED`; SSS reimbursement does not enter the employee cash allocator. A saved-world focused probe also asserts that the existing payroll guard still refuses the cash-funded period. The proposal cannot itself release the payroll guard or authorise payment; an atomic case-linked allocation, actual premium-source binding and component-specific payment allocations are still required. An application before actual SSS award and the lack of a complete evidenced future-premium schedule remain open timing paths; the current adapter refuses rather than guessing.

**PH-SS02 saved advance-plan evidence (29 September 2026; gate pending):** A new PH pay-plan collection creates an immutable revision from a maternity case, a documented monthly full-pay equivalent including a review of qualifying allowances, and twelve saved SSS contribution months. Its transform computes and freezes the SSS candidate, expected or actual contingency basis, application date, 30-day due date, qualifying window and each source row's ID/MSC/paid date/reference. It refuses a missing or incomplete paid-month record, future-dated payments, an unelapsed qualifying window, and an unsupported twelve-month wage-average method. The last method is absent from the writable enum until a dated wage-history calculation exists. Before any employee cash, a corrected case or source month can produce a later immutable plan revision; `advance_status` refuses an older revision. Employee cash movements are pinned to the active plan when recorded, require a bank-credit or signed-voucher file, and cannot be edited after a payment event credits them. Once cash exists, another plan revision and changes to the planned case dates or sourced SSS months refuse. The status query counts only plan-pinned movements with a payment document as an advance recorded by the due date, reports unproved or unlinked cash separately, and compares the later actual SSS award with recorded advance for a required positive or excess true-up. The result is not an SSS award, independent authentication of the document, payment authorisation, employer reimbursement, a computed differential, or a saved payslip. A separate append-only cutoff collection records dated leave partitions and evidence for assessed/projected SSS, PhilHealth and Pag-IBIG employee shares; the full continuous schedule and actual-versus-projected premium check remain unimplemented, so payroll continues to refuse. A plan created after preexisting cash is refused because it cannot freeze the contribution evidence at payment time; that legacy-record reconciliation route remains open.

**PH-SRC05/06 seed citation correction (29 September 2026; tests deferred):** The employer registration/remittance, maternity and kasambahay obligations now cite the official Supreme Court E-Library texts of [RA 11199](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/86333), [RA 11210](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/86567) and [RA 10361](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/51514), instead of the private mirror. All 24 settings' source lists now include those statutes and the SSS [2024-006 business schedule](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/CI-2024-006-Publication.pdf) and [2024-007 household schedule](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/CI-2024-007-Publication.pdf), both operative from January 2025. The 24 SSS, MPF and EC authority texts now distinguish business and household schedules and cite the EC PHP10/PHP30 bracket at MSC PHP14,500/PHP15,000. Values were already present; no calculation was changed. Registration/reporting outputs and production amount probes remain open.

**PH-SS01 household SSS seed correction (29 September 2026; tests deferred):** [SSS Circular 2024-007, pp.1–3](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/CI-2024-007-Publication.pdf) contains a separate household-employer table from January 2025. Below PHP5,000 monthly compensation it has nine brackets, not the business table's single PHP5,000 minimum MSC. The PH `SSS` scheme in all 24 seed versions now selects the household brackets before the business brackets and uses the assessed monthly compensation for the PHP5,000 household-share boundary. Independent table examples: PHP3,000 household compensation → PHP450 employer regular SS, PHP0 employee regular SS, plus PHP10 employer EC (previous seed charged PHP750 employer regular SS); PHP4,800 → PHP750/PHP0 plus PHP10 EC; PHP5,000 → PHP500/PHP250 plus PHP10 EC. These are source-derived expected amounts, not production results. The historical-input, reporting and saved-result branches of PH-SS01 remain open, and no tests have yet been run for this correction.

**PH-SS01 positive-base table coverage (29 September 2026; tests deferred):** All 24 `SSS` ladders now end with a refusal for a positive compensation base and a worker whom the seeded initial/continuing age test treats as covered, if no contribution band matches. [SSS compulsory coverage](https://www.sss.gov.ph/compulsory-coverage/) and the [2025 contribution table](https://www.sss.gov.ph/pay-contribution/) support charging a covered employee in a wage band rather than silently treating a missing band as zero. The focused missing-band probe was authored but has not run. New coverage after age 60, former membership evidence and every other scheme's unmatched branch remain separate open questions.

**Verified correction PH-A3 (issue 19, 2026-09-28):** [RA 6727 s.4](https://lawphil.net/statutes/repacts/ra1989/ra_6727_1989.html) and the [signed NCR-28](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Wage-Order-No.-NCR-28.pdf) §2 (PHP755 from 26 September 2026), §4 (all private-sector minimum wage earners) and §6 (non-payment under RA 6727 s.12) make the floor mandatory; only a board-approved exemption (RA 6727 s.4(c)) lowers it. An NCR five-day worker on PHP7,777.77, hired 17 November 2026, was paid 7,777.77 × 10 ÷ 21.75 = 3,575.99 with only a warning that quoted the 313-day 19,692.92 instead of the 755 × 261 ÷ 12 = 16,421.25 the engine applies. All 24 versions (six active, 18 voided) now carry `wages.block_below_when` (`employment.type != "PART_TIME" && company.facts.minimum_wage_exemption_approved == false`) and the optional entity fact `minimum_wage_exemption_approved` (default false: no approval, no exemption), so the run refuses; the message quotes the floor on the person's own factor. Defaults recorded under the owner rule: a part-timer keeps the warning (the tables state no hours-proportionate floor); a domestic worker is refused like any other covered worker unless the fact is set. The statutory fixture sets the exemption true so the contribution-seam goldens may price sub-floor wages; the NCR-28 golden in `tests/statutory-golden-ph.test.ts` sets it false (7,777.77 and 16,421.24 refuse; 16,421.25 pays 7,550.00; 20,000 is silent; exempted pays 3,575.99 and warns at 16,421.25). Remaining under PH-WG10/11: exemption categories, piece-rate and headcount branches.

**Verified correction PH-S1 (issue 20, 2026-09-28):** the 11 September 2026 NWPC wage-order matrix cited here returned HTTP 404 on 28 September 2026 and every citation now points to the [28 September 2026 matrix](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Latest-Wage-Orders-Matrix-2026-2027-As-of-28-September-2026_updated.pdf) (fetched HTTP 200, 4 pages). It lists NCR-28 effective **26 September 2026** (the old matrix said 21 September), so the matrix now matches the signed order and the sealed 26 September 2026 version; no seed change. The exemption-deadline examples in PH-WG56 (MIMAROPA-13 1 March, RB XI-24 11 May, BARMM-05 4 October 2026) are unchanged in the new matrix, which still lists 17 private-sector sets and no NIR order. Golden: `tests/statutory-golden-ph.test.ts` NCR-28 MWE case — September WTAX base 16,000 × 19 ÷ 22 = 13,818.18 (a 21 September cutover would give 16,000 × 14 ÷ 22 = 10,181.82).

**Verified correction PH-S6 (issue 25, 2026-09-28):** the WTAX authority of every version from 6 January 2026 said the rice subsidy "is de minimis to ₱2,000 a month" (RR 11-2018) while its formula reads `monthly_excess('WTAX.RICE', 2500.0)`. The [RR 29-2025 digest](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RR%20No.%2029-2025%20digest%20FINAL.pdf) (fetched 28 September 2026) states s.2.78.1(A)(3)(d) rice at ₱2,500 a month; its other ceilings (12 VL days, dependants' medical ₱2,000 a semester, uniform ₱8,000, medical ₱12,000, laundry ₱400, awards ₱12,000, gifts ₱6,000, CBA/productivity ₱12,000) match the seed. A transcription fix in place in all 16 WTAX rows from 6 January 2026; the 1 December 2025 and 1–5 January 2026 versions keep ₱2,000 correctly; no formula moved. Golden: `tests/statutory-golden-ph.test.ts` PH-S6 — February 2026, ₱32,000 + ₱2,700 transport + ₱2,400 rice: WTAX base 34,700 (35,100 under ₱2,000), withholding 1,667.55; it also asserts each version's authority names no ceiling other than the one its formula reads.

**Verified correction — untaken meal period (shared-engine defect 2/22, 2026-09-28):** [PD 442 art.85](https://lawphil.net/statutes/presdecs/pd1974/pd_442_1974.html) owes sixty minutes off for meals; art.84(b) counts as hours worked "all time during which an employee is suffered or permitted to work". A meal period not taken is therefore hours worked: the run reports the missed break (`restBreak`) and deducts nothing. The golden harness had deducted it on rest days and holidays, which production never did; its goldens now price every clocked hour.

## Inclusion and exclusion denominator

The review universe is every obligation affecting employment, payroll calculation, statutory deduction/contribution, reporting, leave, safety, records or separation for an employer with a Philippines worksite from **1 December 2025 onward**, including prior service/pay/contribution facts used in that period. It includes national and local enactments; later amendments and supersession; all regional wage-board areas and tranches; ordinary, domestic, apprentice/learner, child, caregiver, security, construction, maritime/media, part-time, piece-rate, foreign-national and public/GOCC worker branches until a primary source excludes them. Human actions such as registration assistance, permit checks, safety controls and authority filings are recorded as obligations even if they are outside a payroll calculator.

Only a branch proved **fund/provider-side with no employer or wage interface**, a **self-paying individual outside employment**, or a date before the operative period with no historical effect may be excluded from implementation. Each exclusion must retain its issuer instrument and reason. This is a scoped denominator definition, **not a completed denominator**: the DOLE/NWPC, BIR, SSS, PhilHealth, HDMF, Official Gazette/local and sector authority indexes plus their signed instruments have not all been reconciled. Accordingly, neither the current count of known IDs nor the six sealed versions can be used as a percentage-compliance denominator.

## Geographic and worker-scope reconciliation

The [NWPC current regional-rate index](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/summary-of-current-regional-daily-minimum-wage-rates-non-agriculture-agriculture-and-other-wage-categories/) and its [28 September 2026 wage-order matrix](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Latest-Wage-Orders-Matrix-2026-2027-As-of-28-September-2026_updated.pdf) show separate regional, sector, area and domestic-worker orders. The NWPC navigation now lists 18 boards including the Negros Island Region; its [NIR page](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/region-nir/) shows a PHP000 placeholder, while the matrix lists only the other 17 sets of private and domestic orders. [RA 12000's IRR](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/101233) includes Negros Occidental, Negros Oriental **and Siquijor**. [NWPC's September 2026 explanation](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/stakeholders-gather-in-dumaguete-city-for-minimum-wage-public-hearing/) says DOLE AO 264 kept Regions VI/VII covering their respective provinces until the NIR board was constituted. The legal effect of that board's later constitution on still-unreplaced orders remains to reconcile; PHP000 is not a rate. Every operative order and tranche from 1 December 2025 needs an official issue/publication/effectivity check. A single region code is insufficient where an order distinguishes municipality class, industry or establishment size. A regional page is an index; its signed orders and amendments are the source for final branch sign-off.

### Signed wage-order disposition, 29 September 2026

The [NWPC matrix dated 28 September 2026](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Latest-Wage-Orders-Matrix-2026-2027-As-of-28-September-2026_updated.pdf) supplies publication and effectivity dates. The linked signed orders are the primary rate and scope instruments; only the seeded rates were checked against their relevant signed sections. The new seed values are **source-derived only**. No run, warning/refusal, saved-payroll or jurisdiction-selection probe was run in this phase. `company.region` remains a manually supplied key, and a region-only table cannot choose an industry, headcount, municipality class or transitional NIR/Sulu worksite. A missing rate must not become a zero or a guessed payable floor.

Private from 6 August, with 1 December 2026 second tranche, varies by area/sector; Sulu transferred to Region IX.                                                                                                                                                                          |

The signed domestic orders used for the seeded rates prescribe a regional monthly floor for occupational household work; most expressly exclude service providers, family drivers and occasional work. The signed orders that address exemption expressly prohibit it. BARMM-DW-02 states a mandatory floor and penalty for noncompliance but has no separate exemption clause. The PH seed's `block_below_when` now refuses a domestic under-floor wage even when `minimum_wage_exemption_approved` is true. Correct domestic classification, actual worksite and agency/principal liability still require product facts and production proofs. The signed orders also require cash payment at least monthly without deductions other than those mandated by law; that employer process is separately tracked under PH-HR02/03.

## Mechanism and evidence queue

Each row must eventually be split into every dated applicability, exception and numerical branch, with actual inputs, independent expected result, production probe, saved result and required report. `Open` means those proofs are incomplete, even where selected examples pass.

## Employment and HR obligation branches

The [DOLE Bureau of Working Conditions handbook, contents and compliance grid](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Workers-Statutory-Monetary-Benefits-Handbook-2024-Edition.pdf) identifies 19 monetary-benefit families, but is a 2024 guide rather than a complete 2025–26 instrument index. The rows below expose each family and additional employment duties. They do not convert guidance into authority: the operative Act, order, amendment, effective date, exclusions and current forms must be checked before certification. A payroll computation and the employer's procedural compliance are separate outputs.

| ID      | Obligation and source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Effective scope / facts → required output                                                                                                                                            | Implemented?              | If not, why?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

## Source-index completeness gates

These are the bounded issuer lists for the December 2025–September 2026 review, not a claim that every item on them is relevant. Each relevant item needs an applicability decision, instrument/publication/effective date, supersession link and affected obligation ID. A handbook or news release cannot substitute for that instrument.

| Issuer / index                                                                                                                                                                        | Required scan and disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Implemented?           | If not, why?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [DOLE Department Orders](https://dole.gov.ph/department-orders/) and [Labor Advisories](https://dole.gov.ph/labor-advisory/)                                                          | 2025/26 orders, amendments and advisories affecting employers: DO 248/248A/248B foreign permits, DO 254 caregivers, DO 259 violence/harassment, LA 12-25 holidays, LA 16-25 13th month, LA 14-26 weather suspension, LA 15-26 poor air, and other applicable instruments.                                                                                     | No [SOURCE 2026-09-29] | Index discovered new families during this pass; full issue text, publication dates, applicability and further items are not reconciled.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| [NWPC regional orders and wage matrix](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/summary-of-current-regional-daily-minimum-wage-rates-non-agriculture-agriculture-and-other-wage-categories/)          | Every signed private/domestic order, predecessor, tranche, locality/industry/size class, exemption and NIR transition from 1 December 2025.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | No [SOURCE 2026-09-29] | All 17 operative legacy board areas now have dated rate entries, but only selected NCR/IV-A values are sealed. Signed clause/exception reconciliation remains incomplete for scanned orders, the FAQ's exemption-guideline link is broken, Sulu private-sector transition and NIR replacement are unresolved, and no nationwide saved-output proof exists.                    |
| [BIR 2025 regulations](https://www.bir.gov.ph/2025-Revenue-Regulations), [2026 regulations](https://www.bir.gov.ph/2026-Revenue-Regulations), circulars, memoranda, forms and annexes | 2025/26 compensation, withholding, PERA, de minimis, retirement, equity/FBT and filing changes; link each amendment to base text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | No [SOURCE 2026-09-29] | PH-AM and PH-WH record selected reads, but base-law, annex and effective-date scan are incomplete. The 2026 public index currently displays “no items” even though signed 2026 regulation PDFs are discoverable (for example [RR 1-2026 digest](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RR%20No.%201-2026%20Digest%20FINAL.pdf)); index-only automation cannot prove completeness. |

This pass checked the [SSS circular list](https://www.sss.gov.ph/sss-circulars/), [PhilHealth's 2025](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/circulars/2025/archives.php) and [2026](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/circulars/2026/archives.php) publication archives, the [DOLE labor-advisory list](https://dole.gov.ph/labor-advisory/), and [NWPC's 2025 wage-report FAQ](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/faqs/). A publication date is not silently substituted for an instrument's commencement date. “No” means the stated branch lacks a verified input-to-saved-output mechanism; it does not mean the app necessarily computes every example incorrectly.

**National holiday denominator read from proclamations.** For 1–31 December 2025, [Proclamation 727](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/7/97992) makes **8, 24 and 31 December special nonworking** and **25 and 30 December regular**. For 2026, [Proclamation 1006](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/7/99678) makes **1 January, 2/3/9 April, 1 May, 12 June, 31 August, 30 November and 25/30 December regular**; **17 February, 4 April, 21 August, 1/2 November and 8/24/31 December special nonworking**; and **25 February special working**. [Proclamations 1189](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/7/101036) and [1264](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/7/101531) add **20 March** and **27 May** as regular Eid holidays. The [PCO news release](https://pco.gov.ph/news_releases/pbbm-issues-proclamation-declaring-regular-holidays-special-non-working-days-for-2026/) mistakenly prints “17 January (Tuesday)” and “24 December (Wednesday)”; the signed Proclamation 1006 records **17 February (Tuesday)** and **24 December (Thursday)**. Local proclamations, later national amendments and actual premium/eligibility mechanisms remain open.

The [PhilHealth 2026 circular archive](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/circulars/2026/archives.php) has 14 listed circulars through 8 September; its [2026 advisory archive](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/advisories/2026/archives.php) adds employer status-response and EPRS payment-channel branches PH-RP06–07. Circulars **2026-0010/0011** expressly concern _self-paying_ members and do not alter an employer's ordinary employee contribution rule. Circulars **0004–0006** concern provider maternity case rates, not employer maternity leave pay. The December 2025 archive item **2025-0028** is the auto-credit scheme for health facilities. The remaining provider/fund-side circulars have no identified payroll formula from their index titles, but full-text sector applicability is still an open issuer gate. The [SSS list](https://www.sss.gov.ph/sss-circulars/) also identifies 2025-006/009/011 and 2026-003/005 loan or benefit instruments: the emergency-loan employer branches are captured at PH-SS05–06 and the calamity-loan employer branch at PH-SS07; the other instruments, publication dates and exact supersession still need full-text disposition. The complete signed [Pag-IBIG Circular 460 annex](https://wcms.dmw.gov.ph/uploads/DMW_ADVISORY_37_2025_01225b9fec.pdf) is now recovered from another Philippine agency; the HDMF issuing site still challenges direct retrieval, and a complete dated HDMF issuer index remains open under PH-HD01–08. The BIR 2026 public index remains incomplete; [RMC 3-2026](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RMC%20No.%203-2026.pdf) and [RMC 54-2026 digest](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RMC%20No.%2054-2026%20Digest.pdf) identify PERA investment products/qualified securities, not an additional employer WTAX credit. Their full-text product-income versus employee-credit interface remains to be checked under PH-PERA01–11.

The [NWPC 28 September matrix](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Latest-Wage-Orders-Matrix-2026-2027-As-of-28-September-2026_updated.pdf) lists NCR-28 (issued 7 September, published 11 September 2026 in the Daily Tribune) as effective **26 September 2026**, with appeal due 21 September and exemption applications due 25 November 2026. That agrees with the [signed NCR-28 §7](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Wage-Order-No.-NCR-28.pdf) (15 days after publication) and [DOLE](https://dole.gov.ph/news/dole-issues-new-ncr-wage-order/). The withdrawn 11 September matrix (now HTTP 404) had shown 21 September; that conflict is resolved (PH-S1). Use **26 September 2026**. The [NWPC NCR page](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/ncr/) still displays NCR-27's nominal 25 July effectivity, but NCR-28's signed recital says NCR-27 never took effect after the status-quo-ante order, TRO and preliminary injunction. The 28 September matrix still lists NCR-27 at 25 July 2026 with the footnote that it "is subject to continuing litigation" (RTC Pasig Branch 152, RTC Navotas Branch 287). Neither presentation may cause a July uplift or a 21 September cutover.

### Cross-region minimum-wage exemption and review process

The [NWPC FAQ](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/faqs/) describes the current exemption criteria and procedure, but its “Rules on Exemption” link resolves to a missing page. The [28 September 2026 order matrix](https://nwpc.dole.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Latest-Wage-Orders-Matrix-2026-2027-As-of-28-September-2026_updated.pdf) supplies order-specific dates. Obtain the operative guideline and each Board decision before treating an application as an approved exemption.

## Shared-tracker migration reconciliation

This is the Philippines disposition of the shared tracker; the rows preserve the original finding IDs so deletion of the shared tracker does not erase open work. All remain **open** for the full stated branch. The existing detailed PH-WH01–20, PH-AM01–10, PH-PERA01–11 and PH-EBET01–13 source dispositions follow below. Earlier probe logs in the realm-root `.tmp/` are transient evidence; durable named probe files and independent legal examples are identified here. **Retained amount evidence.** R45's original production-function probe used the selected dated SSS/MPF/PHIC/HDMF/WTAX rows and independently read [RR 11-2018 §2.79(B)](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RR%20No.%2011-2018.pdf) and [Annex E](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/Annex%20E%20RR%2011-2018.pdf):

| R45 input                                                             | Expected WTAX | Before correction | Present disposition                                                    |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------: | ----------------: | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| July: PHP20,000 basic + PHP10,000 overtime; six prior ordinary months |          PHP0 |       PHP1,045.05 | Corrected in sealed WTAX selections; remaining historical policy open. |
| July: PHP22,000 basic + PHP91,000 bonus                               |          PHP0 |          PHP47.55 | Corrected taxable-regular trigger; saved annual result open.           |
| July: PHP30,000 basic + PHP110,000 bonus                              |   PHP4,007.55 |       PHP4,718.40 | Corrected bracket selection from taxable regular pay.                  |
| January: PHP20,000 basic + PHP10,000 overtime, no history             |   PHP1,045.05 |       PHP1,045.05 | Passing amount control only.                                           |
| July: PHP20,000 basic, no supplement, six prior ordinary months       |          PHP0 |              PHP0 | Passing ordinary control only.                                         |

R46's original July probe showed a PHP30,000 worker with `NOT_REGISTERED` WTAX returned **PHP0 instead of PHP1,007.55**, raising net pay by PHP1,007.55; registered control returned PHP1,007.55. The corrected July gather/build probe verifies ordinary SSS/MPF/EC/PHIC/HDMF/WTAX assessment on unregistered declarations, but does not prove every member class. R47's unexecuted independent annual-adjustment cases are: PHP12,090 annual tax with PHP5,000 valid credit and PHP11,083.05 already withheld → PHP3,993.05 refund and no carry; PHP5,000 tax with PHP10,000 credit and PHP4,000 withheld → PHP4,000 refund and PHP5,000 carry; zero tax with PHP5,000 credit and no withholding → no cash refund and PHP5,000 carry. [RMC 94-2026 Q10](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RMC%20No.%2094-2026_redacted.pdf) gives the employee branch; its Q6 forfeiture for specified return filers must not be substituted for employees.

The six R45 sealed settings were replaced again by the PH-SS01 age correction while keeping all predecessor versions voided. Active setting IDs now have effective starts `2025-12-01`, `2026-01-01`, `2026-01-06`, `2026-02-07`, `2026-04-01` and `2026-09-26`; historical R45/R46 IDs and logs identify earlier reproductions, not current payable versions. Current version lineage and catalogue references must be read from `seed/jurisdiction/PH/`.

## Historical PH production-evidence record

These are the dated R45–R47 production findings and source qualifications as recorded before the later PH SSS age replacement. They preserve independently calculated expected amounts, probe methods, prior version IDs and remaining closure criteria. The current active IDs are in `seed/jurisdiction/PH/`; this record does not make the historical IDs payable again.

### Historical R45 — PH supplementary-pay classification and withholding selection

**Partial correction implemented in six new sealed seed versions; source/history and persistence
work remains open.** The original reproduction found three production mismatches and two passing
amount controls.
[BIR RR 11-2018, s.2.79(B)(3) and (5)(a)](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RR%20No.%2011-2018.pdf)
requires taxable-pay classification after exclusions and mandatory contributions; overtime is
supplementary. The ordinary periodic method selects its bracket from taxable regular compensation,
then applies that bracket to the excess including supplementary pay. Cumulative averaging applies
to the specified supplement/prior-employer cases and persists for the year. The independently read
[Annex E](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/Annex%20E%20RR%2011-2018.pdf) supplies the 2023-onward
tables. The initial review covered selected compensation provisions and examples. The subsequent
PH-WH01–20 review reads all main-text sections and illustrations of the 47-page regulation and
records the separate amendment/annex limits. Pages 27 and 29 were visually checked; Illustration
9's printed narrative arithmetic disagrees with its table, as reconciled independently above.
The complete base-law and amendment inventory remains open.

The six original PH WTAX rows included OVERTIME in `ordinary_on`. Their trigger predicates compared
`ordinary` before mandatory deductions, while the historical helper subtracted them. The ordinary
periodic ladders selected their bracket from the total taxable base. These three mismatches are
separate from MP01's hard-coded historical policy placement and are corrected in the replacements
below.

The original red probe used actual `philippinesCumulativeHistory`, `personContext`, `accumulatePayslip`
and `contribute`, with the then-dated, unedited SSS, SSS_MPF, PHIC, HDMF and WTAX rows. July cases selected
version `81c0ae8c-42d6-5392-be7b-b006158a221c`; January selects
`bb5137fd-d7fd-4a26-8eae-77211521f892`. Workers are resident citizens above the declared NCR wage
floor, with no prior-employer opening and no earlier supplementary trigger. Bonus lines count only
toward WTAX.SPECIAL; the unused PHP90,000 exemption leaves the indicated taxable supplement.

| Original reproduction                                                 | Independent expected WTAX | Before correction | Evidence                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------: | ----------------: | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| July: PHP20,000 basic + PHP10,000 overtime; six prior ordinary months |                      PHP0 |       PHP1,045.05 | Current `ordinary` becomes PHP30,000, hiding the supplement and missing cumulative selection |
| July: PHP22,000 basic + PHP91,000 bonus                               |                      PHP0 |          PHP47.55 | Net regular PHP20,150 is exempt; trigger incorrectly compares pre-deduction PHP22,000        |
| July: PHP30,000 basic + PHP110,000 bonus                              |               PHP4,007.55 |       PHP4,718.40 | Taxable regular PHP27,550 selects 15%; current total PHP47,550 selects the next bracket      |
| January: PHP20,000 basic + PHP10,000 overtime, no history             |               PHP1,045.05 |       PHP1,045.05 | Amount control only: both methods coincide in the first period                               |
| July: PHP20,000 basic, no supplement, six prior ordinary months       |                      PHP0 |              PHP0 | Ordinary no-supplement control                                                               |

Independent arithmetic: the overtime case has six prior taxable PHP18,300 months and current
PHP27,800, averaging PHP19,657.14. The PHP22,000 case has six prior PHP20,150 months and current
PHP21,150, averaging PHP20,292.86. Both averages fall below Annex E's PHP20,833 monthly threshold.
For the third case, `(27,550 - 20,833 + 20,000) × 15% = 4,007.55`; supplementary PHP20,000 is
below taxable regular PHP27,550, so this case does not trigger cumulative averaging.

Log: `.tmp/payroll-ph-cumulative-trigger-probe.log`: **five cases, three mismatches, two controls**.
Registered statuses pass the production status validator; scalar salary and computed monthly basic
are asserted. The first diagnostic stopped when migration changed salary from a money object to a
scalar; the completed run uses the current scalar input. Source hashes stayed unchanged during that
run. This is contribution/history evidence, not saved-input, wage pricing or full-run evidence.

Why the existing probes missed these cases: their low-pay trigger uses PHP20,000 before deductions,
already below the threshold; their larger bonus exceeds both regular comparands. They do not
exercise the net-only threshold, overtime classification, or a supplementary payment crossing the
regular-pay bracket without triggering averaging. A first-period amount match cannot prove correct
method selection, as the passing January control demonstrates.

#### Implemented seed correction and evidence

Six new sealed snapshots retain the original effective intervals and identify their predecessor
through `cloned_from_id`. The original roots are voided, and every original child row is unchanged.
The replacement WTAX rows exclude overtime, night differential and taxable leave cash-out from
regular compensation; the existing maternity exclusion also applies to that regular base.
Current trigger predicates compare net regular/supplementary values. Ordinary table selection uses
net regular compensation, with explicit monthly/semi-monthly/weekly guards. Rule amount formulas,
tax rates, assessed-base exemptions, annualisation precedence and all non-WTAX scheme content are
unchanged.
These are corrections to the same legal periods, not new statutory commencement dates.

`tests/ph-withholding-selection.probe.test.ts`: **15 tests pass**. Eight monthly production-function

The synthetic fixture now selects only operative snapshots and their catalogues. Four existing
probe files required 18 ID-only line updates to reference the new copies; no golden monetary
expectation changed. The 13th-month probe first reproduced the refusal of an old voided catalogue
reference (`.tmp/payroll-ph-catalogue-selection.log`), then passed with the replacement reference.
`statutory_contributions.json.gz` uses the existing compressed-law reader because retaining both

`.tmp/payroll-ph-r45-integrity.json`; original children are unchanged, IDs/references are valid,
and new non-WTAX policy matches its predecessor. No `src/**`, customer-bank, generated-artifact or
dependency change was made; no E2E/broad suite ran.

**Remaining closure work:** MP01's historical trigger constants still live in source. Ambiguous
legacy ordinary amounts need explicit reconciliation. Mixed-cadence conversion needs authority
and execution evidence; equal-supplement, year-reset and termination interactions need their
remaining cases. The selected successful nonresident/annualisation probes do not exhaust those
