Saved-input/version activation, settlement
and deployed-artifact evidence remain R05. R45 therefore stays open despite the corrected current
selection and passing probes.

### Historical R46 — PH registration status silently waived mandatory charges

**Philippine statutory-charge undercharge corrected; shared liability review remains open.**
[RR 11-2018 s.9 / RR 2-98 s.2.79.2](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RR%20No.%2011-2018.pdf)
expressly requires table withholding where the employee fails or refuses to file the registration
application. A NOT_REGISTERED declaration does not establish a tax exemption.
The [SSS Act and implementing rules](https://www.sss.gov.ph/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/IRR-RA11199-SS-Act-of-2018_2.pdf)
put compulsory employee coverage from the first day of employment;
[SSS employer guidance](https://www.sss.gov.ph/employer-er/) requires employee and employer
contributions, EC and reporting despite uncompleted registration.
[PhilHealth employer guidance](https://www.philhealth.gov.ph/partners/employers/) requires
premium remittance and reporting, and [HDMF Circular 275](https://www.pagibigfund.gov.ph/document/pdf/circulars/provident/HDMF%20Circular%20275%20-%20Implementing%20Guidelines%20on%20Employer%20Registration%20Contribution%20and%20Remittance.pdf)
states mandatory employer coverage. The applicable scheme's eligibility rules still decide
whether a particular employee is covered; registration alone cannot waive the liability.

The former `src/lib/payroll/run/contribute.ts` gate emitted zero before rule selection whenever
`status.kind === 'NOT_REGISTERED'` and base was positive. It caused the same class of error as
R43's mandatory MY HRD bypass. The scheme model now declares `unregistered_action` (`SKIP` or
`ASSESS`); the engine applies the ladder for `ASSESS` and warns that registration is incomplete.
All 36 current PH scheme rows (six mandatory schemes in six versions) set `ASSESS`
in new sealed replacements of the R45 snapshots. The prior versions and every child remain as
voided history. The unchanged default `SKIP` still requires a scheme-by-scheme legal review;
it cannot be taken as proof that other mandatory obligations are exempt.

The read-only probe uses `createStatutoryWorld` → actual `gatherPayrollRun` → `buildPayrollRun`,
July 2026, NCR, a resident citizen with a full month, no extra pay and no opening history.
Each WTAX status passes production `statutoryFactStatusFault`. REGISTERED supplies a synthetic
TIN; NOT_REGISTERED supplies the reason that the employee has not filed the required application.
Other mandatory registrations were unchanged in this historical reproduction. Its selected sealed version was
`6e74b01c-72bc-5a68-887d-b85f91772e9d`.

| Basic salary | WTAX status    | Independent expected WTAX | Before R46 WTAX | Before R46 net | Historical result   |
| ------------ | -------------- | ------------------------: | --------------: | -------------: | ------------------- |
| PHP30,000    | REGISTERED     |               PHP1,007.55 |     PHP1,007.55 |   PHP26,542.45 | Control passes      |
| PHP30,000    | NOT_REGISTERED |               PHP1,007.55 |            PHP0 |      PHP27,550 | Failure; no warning |
| PHP20,000    | REGISTERED     |                      PHP0 |            PHP0 |      PHP18,300 | Control passes      |
| PHP20,000    | NOT_REGISTERED |                      PHP0 |            PHP0 |      PHP18,300 | Control passes      |

Independent PHP30,000 arithmetic: SSS PHP1,000 + MPF PHP500 + PHIC PHP750 + HDMF PHP200
= PHP2,450 mandatory employee contributions; `(30,000 - 2,450 - 20,833) × 15% = 1,007.55`.
All four runs return no warnings. Log: `.tmp/payroll-ph-registration-probe.log` records **one
mismatch and three controls**, the selected version, amounts and source hashes. Hashes of the
four observed production modules were unchanged around execution. The diagnostic exits nonzero
for the legal mismatch; this is a reproduced product error, not a failed import or invalid salary
shape. It does not establish saved-collection validation, database persistence or deployed output.

**Post-fix evidence, 26 September:** all 36 active PH scheme rows declare `ASSESS`.
The durable `statutory-audit-ph.test.ts` probe validates the NOT_REGISTERED input and executes
the real gather/build path in July 2026. Each of the six schemes is probed separately and all
six together at PHP20,000 and PHP30,000. At PHP30,000 the independently derived shares are
SSS **1,000/2,000**, mandatory provident fund **500/1,000**, EC **0/30**, PhilHealth
**750/750**, HDMF **200/200**, and WTAX **1,007.55/0** (employee/employer PHP). Each
NOT_REGISTERED case matches the registered charge and net pay and emits a registration warning;
at PHP20,000 both statuses legally withhold zero WTAX. Four
`statutory-coverage-ph.test.ts` cases now assert that incomplete SSS registration does not waive
SS/MPF, while separate age/first-liability cases still skip people genuinely outside coverage.
`contribution-banding.test.ts` checks that `ASSESS` retains other required declarations and
`SKIP` remains distinct. The R46 clone
integrity check confirms each of 311 child rows is unchanged apart from IDs/settings pointers
and the 36 registration policies. All three PH bank ad-hoc requests resolve to the matching sealed
catalogue effective on their event date. `pnpm test:node` passes **2,107/2,107**, and
`pnpm exec bolt test -- tests/integration/payroll.test.ts` passes **7/7** with the refreshed
bank pack. The artifact refresh omitted only the type stage while the concurrent page migration
keeps the normal build red.

**Remaining R46 closure:** probe every PH effective interval through calculation, registration
and TIN variants, annual/final reconciliation, saved-record execution and remittance output.
Audit the registration gate for every mandatory scheme in the other jurisdictions. MY HRD's
compulsory branch now assesses the levy in all active versions; its employer-level registration
history and category remain R43. Retain lawful optional
non-election cases. The default `SKIP` is still an unverified policy for those schemes; R46
stays open until each has a sourced disposition and production evidence.

### Historical R47 — PH PERA certificate input, annual credit and unused-credit carry

**Confirmed input/policy gap; no accepted-input amount failure is claimed.** PERA has distinct
employer-contribution exemption and employee tax-credit mechanisms. PH-AM03 concerns the former;
the employee's approved PERA Tax Credit Certificate (TCC) reduces tax due, not taxable wages.
[RR 2-2022 s.4](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RR%20No.%202-2022.pdf),
[RMC 139-2020 Q6–7](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RMC%20No.%20139-2020%20%281%29.pdf)
and [RMC 94-2026 Q10, employee row](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/BIR/pdf/RMC%20No.%2094-2026_redacted.pdf)
require the employer's year-end adjustment, refund of excess tax actually withheld/remitted and
carry of credit exceeding annual tax into the next taxable year. The carry requirement already
appears in the 2020 circular; it was not first introduced in August 2026. A refund of prior tax
is distinct from cashing out unused credit. Q6 of the 2026 circular forfeits excess on the specified
self-employed/overseas-Filipino return; it must not override Q10's employee carry branch.

**Current trace and probe.** All six active PH WTAX declarations have no elections and no rule
for PERA credit or rebate consumption. The statutory-status model has prior-employer opening
amounts, generic elections and deductions, but no declared certificate identity, issue/expiry,
utilisation or unused-credit carry contract. `opening.rebate` represents earlier-employer
rebatable payments; it is not a declaration of an available PERA certificate. The contribution
engine records `rule.rebate` separately from `rule.employee`; a rebate field alone does not
subtract a tax credit. An arbitrary rate override or invented prior tax payment cannot substitute
for the legal input and its history.

The actual `employment_statutory_facts` collection transform, using in-memory reads of each
active seeded WTAX row, accepts six ordinary REGISTERED controls and refuses twelve proposed
PERA inputs: `elections.pera_tcc_amount` is undeclared, and deduction category `PERA_TCC` is unused.
These names are diagnostic candidate inputs, not an existing supported contract. The refusal
is correct for undeclared keys; the finding is the missing legal capability/declaration, not
that validation should accept arbitrary keys. Source inspection confirms no alternate PERA
declaration/rule in these versions. Log `.tmp/payroll-ph-pera-input-probe.log` records eighteen
cases and source/seed hashes stable around execution. No schema, source, seed or database write
was made, and no credited payroll output was manufactured by bypassing validation.

**Required inputs and policy:** contributor/payment scope, submitted approved certificate and
reference, qualifying contribution year, amount issued/available, issued date, cancellation/
utilisation state, prior applications and remaining carry. Apply the dated validity rule:
[RR 7-2023](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RR%20No.%207-2023.pdf) and RMC 94-2026 Q3
give five years for certificates issued on or after **27 July 2023**; do not assign that rule
to older certificates automatically. Preserve certificates for inspection and their details in
2316/Alphalist under [RMO 17-2022 III.7](https://bir-cdn.bir.gov.ph/local/pdf/RMO%20No.%2017-2022.pdf).
Application/approval and official authenticity/CLAIMED status remain external facts; payroll
must consume and retain them without pretending to issue BIR certificates.

**Independent closure cases, not yet executed:**

| Annual tax before credit | Valid available employee credit | Tax already withheld/remitted |                   Expected final adjustment | Unused credit carried |
| -----------------------: | ------------------------------: | ----------------------------: | ------------------------------------------: | --------------------: |
|                   12,090 |                           5,000 |                     11,083.05 | Refund 3,993.05; annual net liability 7,090 |                     0 |
|                    5,000 |                          10,000 |                         4,000 |        Refund 4,000; annual net liability 0 |                 5,000 |
|                        0 |                           5,000 |                             0 |                   No tax and no cash refund |                 5,000 |

Use an already issued, valid certificate submitted for the adjustment; a contribution made in
the current year does not by itself prove that a certificate has been issued. Add zero-credit,
partial utilisation, duplicate reference, cancellation, older certificate, expiry boundary,
next-year utilisation, reversal/rebuild and mixed-income/substituted-filing cases. Reconcile
tax actually remitted and refunded separately from certificate credit so neither can be used
twice. Employer-contribution exemption/caps require separate PH-AM03 cases. New dated policy
must use sealed replacement versions and independent goldens. Source changes remain on hold
until the user confirms migration copy-back; targeted admission, calculation, history and
saved-input probes remain required before closure.

## Source and coverage review — 2026-09-28

Independent source review of this register and of the sources `seed/jurisdiction/PH/` cites. Each finding was re-checked by a second pass before it was applied, and the corrected reading is the one recorded. Nothing below is certified; every row stays `No`. Seed and `src/lib/statutory_sources.ts` defects are recorded here as open rows; neither file was changed in this pass.

- **Sources replaced in place (amendment history kept in the row):** PH-HL01 now names PhilHealth Circular 2020-0005 (Revision 1), the schedule PA2025-0002 ¶1 itself cites, instead of relying on the superseded 2019-0009 schedule. PH-SS01 adds the signed CI 2024-006 publication PDF. The five Labor Code links (PH-HR01, 03, 15, 19, 20) are now labelled as the original PD 442 numbering, which is what E-Library 26/25306 serves.
- **Corrected content:** PH-HR35's under-15 night bar is 8 p.m.–6 a.m. under RA 9231 s.12-A(3). The earlier “no work after 10 p.m.” is the bar for ages 15–17. PH-HR24 now states the age-band hour caps and the s.12-C trust fund, which is the parent's or guardian's duty, not the employer's.
- **New rows:** PH-SRC01–09 (stale or non-official seed sources and a blocked renumbered Labor Code); PH-HR41 (payroll content and task-work intervals); PH-HR42 (NIRC s.235 five-year tax records); PH-WH21–23 (EOPT withholding timing and channels, micro/small penalties, and the s.57(B) exemption that does not reach compensation withholding); PH-HR43 (probation); PH-HR44–45 (kasambahay rest periods and termination money); PH-HR46–47 (wage distortion, money-claims prescription); PH-HR48–49 (night workers, telecommuting); PH-HR50 (local special non-working days by Republic Act).
- **Still blocked on 28 Sep 2026:** dole.gov.ph, bwc.dole.gov.ph, the ncr/ble/ro7/oshc.dole.gov.ph subdomains and officialgazette.gov.ph returned 403, and bwc/dole served a Cloudflare challenge to the in-app browser. The result: LA 12-26 and the DOLE renumbered Labor Code are unread. (Later that day LA 06-20 was read from a Wayback capture and DO 202-19 was found superseded by the Revised IRR on E-Library; see PH-SRC07 and PH-HR49.) www.pagibigfund.gov.ph serves a Google reCAPTCHA page, which was not bypassed. So no Pag-IBIG issuance after Circular 460 was verified. Retried 28 Sep 2026 (round 4): www.dole.gov.ph, bwc.dole.gov.ph and officialgazette.gov.ph still 403 to curl; the in-app browser still stops at the Cloudflare "Performing security verification" page, which was not bypassed; Wayback holds no capture of the BWC Labor Code PDF. LA 12-26 and the renumbered Labor Code stay unread (only a non-official mirror of LA 12-26 was found, not used). The Wayback copy of Circular 460 is truncated at 1,048,576 bytes and is not used.
- **Not verified in this pass:** RA 10151 and RA 11165 were not opened, so their article and section pins are unverified. The claims that no 2026 SSS circular changes a rate and that no 2026 Republic Act through RA 12325 changes wages or contributions were not re-verified. The publication dates, and therefore the effective dates, of the 2025 local-holiday Acts were not checked.

The payroll interface is transfer and maternity pay continuity.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Closed (sourced exclusion from the calculator) [EXTERNAL 2026-09-29] | [RA 10151](https://elibrary.judiciary.gov.ph/thebookshelf/showdocs/2/37482) read 28 Sep 2026 (approved 21 June 2011; Manila Bulletin 27 June 2011; in force 15 days after publication, s.11). It inserts Labor Code Book III Title III Chapter V, Arts. 154–161 in its own numbering: coverage of work at night during a period of not less than seven consecutive hours including midnight to 5 a.m., fixed by the Secretary, excluding agriculture, stock raising, fishing, maritime and inland navigation (Art. 154); free health assessment on request (155); first aid, rest quarters, transport (156); transfer when unfit, else "the same benefits as other workers who are unable to work" (157); a 16-week alternative to night work around childbirth (158); compensation "shall recognize the exceptional nature of night work" (159, no figure). No numeric pay rule is added: the payroll figure is the art.86 10% night differential, goldened. Transfer, assessment and facility duties are HR processes with no payroll input. Decision reason closed (`tests/holiday-calendar.test.ts`).
